HOCKEY. ----- LANCASHIRE V. YORKSHIRE. This match was decided on Saturday on the ground of the Rersal Cricke.t Club.b!fore a rather meagre atten- dance. Result :—Yorkshire, two goals ; Lancashire, none. LA TCHFORD " " V FRODSHAM. This match took place at Frodsham on Saturday. During the first half of the game Latchford did most of the pressing, but failed to get the ball through, on account of the good defence of the home team, The interval arrived with nothing scored. Shortly after the resumption of play, Frodsham rushed down the field, and put the ball through, after a scramble in the goal- mouth. Latchford took matters to the other end, and shortly afterwards Knight scored a good goal for the visitors, placing the teams equal. Towards the close of Play the light was very bad, and the match was stopped Eve minutes from time. Result —Latchford " A," one goal ; Frodsham, one goal. tore Is Security in ,RTER'S

Horse Fair. The last horse fair of the Near was held on Satur- day on the vacant space behind the Blue Coat School. There was only a small attendance of buyers, and tire animals presented for sale were scarce and of very poor quality. This was the worst fair which has been held in Warrington for some time.

Boscow, T Farrimond, T Howard, T Holeroft, and F Bosch. From iradford A L Bairstow, J Grandage, and H Holden. From Hunslet, F R Howarth, F W Pawson, F W Rose. and H C Gladwin. From Swinton, P Wilkinson. From Brough- ton Rangers, D M Fielding. More Professional Offences. Severe Punishments. Seabseqnent to the proceedings reported above, there was a meeting of the Committee of the Northern Union, presided over by Mr J H Houghton, of St Helens, It was of a protracted character, and the business was of more than ordinary interest. As will be seen from the re- port below, a number of penalties were imposed for breaches of the laws relating to professionalism. In practically all the cases the players had not complied with the employment rule. The fines in two instances—those in which the Hot- beck and Keighley clubs are involved—are somewhat serious; but they will no doubt be of less importance to the clubs than the deduction of Cons letition. points, which will follow in the ordinary course. All the fines imposed must be paid OR or before February lit next, under threat ef worse penalties still. The decisions of tha Committee in these matters may be thus summarised The Keighley Club was fined £2O for breach of Professions LaW 1, Sub-seation C and 13 Patrick, a player of the cl oh was suspended in connection with the case up ts May 1 1902. The Holbeck Club was fines £2O for a breach of Law 1; Sub-Section C. and I` Goodfellow, the threequarter back concerned, was suepende4 s:ne die. SECRETARY'S NOVEL SPPLICATION. Mr AM Ricketts,who lately cause from Cardiff to be secre- tary to the Halifax Club. made a personal application for permission to play as an snoatour with the Halifax team. The committee, however decided not to entertain the appli- cation. THE CASE OF OATH. The Committee of the Yorkshire nenior Competition brought before the Union the question of Gath's registration as a Lancashire Senior Competition players. The Com- mittee, after careful consideration, decided that in order to comply with the various competition transfer laws a trans- fer should be obtained for Gah, both from the Batley and Heckmondwike Clnbi. The Kingston Rovers Club not having obtained a trans- fer from Heckmondwike must no so before again allowing Gath to take part for them in Lancashire Senior C =peti- tion matches. THE PROFESSIONAL ERTB•CSMMITTEE. An application was made by the Yorkshire Senior Com- petition for a representative on the Professional Sub- committee. but the secretary (Mr J Platt) was instructed to reply that the Union had never recognised competitions in the appointment of the Committee, and that they regretted they were unable to grant the request. RE-IssTATEMENT OF PLAYERS. T Hammond. of Horbury-, who was suspended by the Yorkshire Rugby Union about four years ago for misconduct, was reinstated from date. W Wyford, of Outwood Church, who was sent off the field for striking an opponent in the cup ties last season, was re- instated as from January 1 next: THE TORII ITNIoN GOES OVER. The York and District Rugby Union and the Wallsend Club were elected to membership. It was resolved that in future only refusals of permits be published, the reasons for such refusal being given. Suspensions for unruly conduct on the field : B Flannagan (Runcorn) till December 14. M Gledhill (Salforci) till December 28. W Burrell (Hebden Bridge)tAll December 14. G Overton (Hull Stoneferry) till January 31. F Miller (Brighouse) till December 21. Smith (Brighouse " A '') till December 23. J E Brans (Swinton), till December 7. W Nutter (Brighouse) till December 21.

MUNICIPAL TRADING Mr. Norbury Williams and the Challenge of H Ingram, Ltd. TO THE EDITOR OF THE EXAMINER. Sir, Referring to the challenge of Messrs H. Ingeam Ltd., In the Warrington papers of Saturday last, offering to give £2O to the Infirmary if they are right on condition that I should give £2O to the Infirmary if I am wrong I now set out afresh paragraphs 24, 25, and 23 con- tained in my letter of the 15th inst. addressed to Mr. Alderman F. W. Monks. 24.—" I find that you are chairman of the Electric Lighting' committee and that on October 17th last your committee resolved to purchase motors, and to hire them out to ratepayers who might desire to use them. This course was approved by the council so recently as October 29th. Now notice what happened in this committee of 'high moral tone.' A Warrington firm whom we will call A. B. and Co., asked your Electric Lighting department for motive power. On the very name day, the foreman of a certain company named 'H. Ingram, Limited,' presented himself at A. B• and Co.'s works, and coolly asked for the 'order for motors, and stated that Ingram's bad had an intimation from a certain official of the Corporation Electricity department that A. B. and Co., had that day inquired for motive power. Not only did this foreman of Ingram's Lirniteo. call, but the company wrote a letter to A. B. and Co., on the same day. It corn- mences ' Hearing you have made an application to the Werth gton Corporation,' and so on. I can only say that, if this information had been given in Manchester, the officit 1, however exalted or highly paid, would have been sent about his business. 25. "Now who is this firm of 'H. Ingram Limited, • which is so favoured by the ' high moral tone Electricity committee as to receive early and ex elusive information from the committee's °Hide giving the name of the firm asking for rnotiv power? Its list of shareholders is b3fore me now and,after perusing this list,l am not at all aurpireep that 'lngram's are picked out in this way anp highly favoured. 28. "Pray what right had 'lngram's to be preferred above other moth-dealers ? Are there not a hun- dred firms from whom motors can be obtained? And you tell me you see nothing improper in tois.' Then I say again you are unfit to be a public repre- sentative, and to e2end the money of the Warring- ton ratepayers." Now I undertake to prove the above fants and as soon as H. Ingram 'Ltd have deposited £2O with the manager of Part's Bank, Warrington, I will deposit the like amount on condition that Mr. Wainwright, Arbitra- tor and Valuer, of Liverpool is appointed to d:cide the question. I make tbe above condition is to the deposit of this £2O before we proceed with the matter because I do not know whether under this Company's Articles of Association they are authorised to spend the money of their shareholders in this particular manner. I confirm wbat I stated about' the Zillah Shipping &- Carrying Co., Ltd., except the point that Mr. Brough was its chairman. This statement was originally not mine at all. A gentleman in the meeting shouted out "He is chairman." I accepted his word thinking he would know betcer than I. but be was wrong and con- sequently so was I.—Yours truly, S. NORBURY WILLIAMS. The following is the text of the challenge to Mr. Williams issued through the press last Week by H. Ingram Ltd. A Challenge. We challenge him to prove (1) That A. Band ;Co., made an application for electric power to the tCorpor- ation. (2) that we were informed of this by a member of :the Etectricity Committee or by an official of the Electricity Department. If Mr Williams can prove these charges we will pay £2O to the Warrington Infirmary on condition that he pays a similar sum if he fails in his proof. We would suggest that he and we should each name a magistrate of the Borough and that they should name a third to act as the umpire. If this challenge is declined the public will know what conclusion to come to. Yours truly, H. INGRAM, LIMITED. Councillor Forshaw Replies to Coun cillor F. R. Roberts—" Family" Companies.
